We need to audit the provided SQL. The question: "Which decade of publication (e.g., 1980s) has the highest average rating among decades with at least 10 distinct books that have been rated? Return the decade with the highest average rating."

SQL provided:

```sql
WITH extracted AS (
    book_id,
    TRY_CAST(REGEXP_EXTRACT(details, '([0-9]{4})', 1) AS BIGINT) AS pub_year,
    rating_number AS rating
  FROM books_info
  WHERE rating_number IS NOT NULL
    AND details IS NOT NULL
),
decade_agg AS (
    CAST(pub_year / 10 AS INTEGER) * 10 AS decade,
    AVG(rating) AS avg_rating,
    COUNT(DISTINCT book_id) AS book_cnt
  FROM extracted
  WHERE pub_year IS NOT NULL
  GROUP BY decade
  HAVING COUNT(DISTINCT book_id) >= 10
)
SELECT decade
FROM decade_agg
ORDER BY avg_rating DESC
LIMIT 1;
```

PROPOSED FIX: WITH latest_release AS (
    SELECT pi.Name,
           pi.Version,
           ROW_NUMBER() OVER (
               PARTITION BY pi.Name
               ORDER BY TRY_CAST(json_extract_string(pi.VersionInfo, '$.Ordinal') AS BIGINT) DESC
           ) AS rn
    FROM packageinfo pi
    WHERE pi.System = 'NPM'
      AND TRY_CAST(json_extract_string(pi.VersionInfo, '$.IsRelease') AS BOOLEAN) = true
),
stars AS (
    SELECT TRY_CAST(REPLACE(REGEXP_EXTRACT(Project_Information, '([0-9]+) stars', 1), ',', '') AS BIGINT) AS stars,
           LOWER(REGEXP_EXTRACT(Project_Information, 'project ([^ ]+)', 1)) AS repo_name
    FROM project_info
    WHERE Project_Information IS NOT NULL
)
SELECT lr.Name,
       lr.Version,
       s.stars
FROM latest_release lr
JOIN project_packageversion pv
  ON lr.Name = pv.Name AND lr.Version = pv.Version
JOIN stars s
  ON LOWER(pv.ProjectName) = s.repo_name
WHERE lr.rn = 1
ORDER BY s.stars DESC
LIMIT 5;[0m

```sql
WITH extracted AS (
    book_id,
    CAST(REGEXP_EXTRACT(details, '([0-9]{4})', 1) AS BIGINT) AS pub_year,
    rating_number AS rating
  FROM books_info
  WHERE rating_number IS NOT NULL
    AND details IS NOT NULL
),
decade_agg AS (
    CAST(pub_year / 10 AS INTEGER) * 10 AS decade,
    AVG(rating) AS avg_rating,
    COUNT(DISTINCT book_id) AS book_cnt
  FROM extracted
  WHERE pub_year IS NOT NULL
  GROUP BY decade
  HAVING COUNT(DISTINCT book_id) >= 10
)
SELECT decade
FROM decade_agg
ORDER BY avg_rating DESC
LIMIT 1;
```

  "error_analysis": "The previous query violated DuckDB's rule against using functions in join predicates (LOWER(pv.ProjectName)). It also needed a clear step to select only the latest release per package. The fix is to compute lower\u2011cased repo names in a CTE and join on plain column equality, preserving the latest\u2011release filter.",
  "thought_process": "Mapped prior dead ends: function in join predicate (join on LOWER) and no explicit CTE for lower\u2011casing. Pivoted to a structural change by adding a CTE that pre\u2011computes the lower\u2011cased ProjectName, then join on that column. Retained ROW_NUMBER logic for latest release, used TRY_CAST for safe numeric conversion, and limited to top 5 by stars.",
  "probe_sql": null,
  "sql": "WITH latest_release AS (\n    SELECT pi.Name,\n           pi.Version,\n           ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY pi.Name ORDER BY TRY_CAST(json_extract_string(pi.VersionInfo, '$.Ordinal') AS BIGINT) DESC) AS rn\n    FROM packageinfo pi\n    WHERE pi.System = 'NPM'\n      AND TRY_CAST(json_extract_string(pi.VersionInfo, '$.IsRelease') AS BOOLEAN) = true\n),\npv_lower AS (\n    SELECT Name,\n           Version,\n           LOWER(ProjectName) AS repo_name_pv\n    FROM project_packageversion\n),\nproj_info AS (\n    SELECT TRY_CAST(REPLACE(REGEXP_EXTRACT(Project_Information, '([0-9]+) stars', 1), ',', '') AS BIGINT) AS stars,\n           LOWER(REGEXP_EXTRACT(Project_Information, 'project ([^ ]+)', 1)) AS repo_name\n    FROM project_info\n    WHERE Project_Information IS NOT NULL\n)\nSELECT lr.Name,\n       lr.Version,\n       p.stars\nFROM latest_release lr\nJOIN pv_lower pv ON lr.Name = pv.Name AND lr.Version = pv.Version\nJOIN proj_info p ON pv.repo_name_pv = p.repo_name\nWHERE lr.rn = 1\nORDER BY p.stars DESC\nLIMIT 5;"
}

We need to audit the provided SQL against the question and schema, using directives. The SQL is:

```sql
WITH extracted AS (
        book_id,
        rating_number AS rating,
        CAST(SUBSTR(TRIM(details, ' .'), -4) AS INTEGER) AS pub_year
    FROM books_info
    WHERE rating_number IS NOT NULL
      AND details IS NOT NULL
),
decades AS (
        rating,
        pub_year,
        CAST(pub_year / 10 AS INTEGER) * 10 AS decade
    FROM extracted
    WHERE pub_year IS NOT NULL
)
SELECT decade
FROM decades
GROUP BY decade
HAVING COUNT(DISTINCT book_id) >= 10
ORDER BY AVG(rating) DESC
LIMIT 1;
```

We need to check for structural flaws: alias usage, missing columns, grouping, division guard, etc.

rating: 4 | title: A wonderful adventure in France | text: I loved this book all the way until the end. I have recently discovered that the author is intending to release another book, and from what I understand it will continue where this one left off. I am thankful for this, as the way this book ended was definitely a negative for me. I wanted to know more! What happened to the author, did she learn what she came to learn? Did she get the job she was hoping for? So many questions.<br /><br />From the beginning of Linda's book, it is easy to love her. She is open, honest and definitely has the type of personality you want your heroine to have, whether the book is fiction or reality. I couldn't help but root for Linda throughout her trials with her host family, even when I thought she acted as a bit of a brat herself. Listening to her internal thoughts about what she went through and her desire to achieve her goals made this book feel close to my heart.<br /><br />I applaud the adventurous spirit of the author and her decision to keep journal entries from that chapter of her life. What great material to have later to inspire a book! While I enjoyed the entire memoir, my favourite part of this book would have to be the author's descriptions of the many places she visited and the people she met along the way. While I think she could definitely have made a better impression on the family she worked for if she had been honest about her lack of French language skills from the beginning, she is a pioneer. Her drive and desire to learn the language from those in the actual country was inspiring. Not many people would have been gutsy enough to do what Linda did.<br /><br />I also particularly enjoyed the relationship between Linda and Antoine. The Kind heart of the author was apparent in her actions toward the children, even when she struggled with them.<br /><br />There is some romance, and I appreciated the way the author handled it. While sex scenes are not necessarily automatically offensive, there is something about memoirs that makes me uncomfortable if they are filled with them. This author manages to hint at her experiences without going into too much detail, leaving the reader to decide exactly what happened. Great writing.<br /><br />When you step into reading this book, you learn a lot about French customs, French cuisine and wine. You also get the opportunity to brush up on your French skills a bit and get to step outside yourself and live an adventure through the eyes of the author. I read this book in one sitting with very few breaks in between and found myself enthralled in the sights, sounds and atmosphere of this memoir.<br /><br />In the end, I was disappointed that the book ended. I could have kept reading about Linda's experiences without stopping anytime in the foreseeable future. The end of the book leaves many unanswered questions, that I am assuming the author will be answering in her next book. Were this a standalone, I would be very disappointed with the way the book ended, but with the knowledge that she is writing another book to continue with her story, I can't fault this one. One thing is for sure, if you read this book, you will be anxiously awaiting the next.<br /><br />I would recommend this to anyone who likes books about travel, memoirs or just a good story that came from the recollections and heart of the author. Read it, you will be glad you did. Overall, this was a VERY enjoyable read that gave me many reasons to wish I could drop everything and head off to France! | review_time: 2013-05-05 10:47:00 | helpful_vote: 1 | verified_purchase: 0 | purchase_id: purchaseid_190

  "hierarchy_audit": "Anchor fact: project_packageversion (NPM packages). Join to project_info for star counts. Use CTE to select latest version per package, then join star counts, filter System='NPM', order by stars desc, limit 5.",
  "sql_vs_retrieval_decision": "SQL query will compute top 5 packages by GitHub stars using extracted star count from Project_Information and latest version per package.",
  "thought_process": "Grain: one row per package (latest version) with its star count. Measures: star count extracted via regex. Filters: System='NPM', latest version only. Grouping: by package name. Join on package name. Use ROW_NUMBER to pick latest version. Order by stars desc, limit 5.",
  "probe_sql": "",
  "sql": "WITH latest_pkg AS (\n  SELECT Name, Version,\n         ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY Name ORDER BY Version DESC) AS rn\n  FROM project_packageversion\n  WHERE System = 'NPM'\n), latest_version AS (\n  SELECT Name, Version\n  FROM latest_pkg\n  WHERE rn = 1\n), star_counts AS (\n  SELECT pi.Name,\n         TRY_CAST(REPLACE(REGEXP_EXTRACT(pi.Project_Information, '([0-9]+)[ \\t]+stars', 1), ',', '') AS BIGINT) AS stars\n  FROM project_info pi\n)\nSELECT lv.Name,\n       lv.Version,\n       sc.stars\nFROM latest_version lv\nJOIN star_counts sc ON sc.Name = lv.Name\nORDER BY sc.stars DESC\nLIMIT 5;"

```sql
WITH latest_pkg AS (
  SELECT Name, Version,
         ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY Name ORDER BY Version DESC) AS rn
  FROM project_packageversion
  WHERE System = 'NPM'
), latest_version AS (
  FROM latest_pkg
  WHERE rn = 1
), star_counts AS (
  SELECT pi.Name,
         TRY_CAST(REPLACE(REGEXP_EXTRACT(pi.Project_Information, '([0-9]+)[ \t]+stars', 1), ',', '') AS BIGINT) AS stars
  FROM project_info pi
)
SELECT lv.Name,
       lv.Version,
       sc.stars
FROM latest_version lv
JOIN star_counts sc ON sc.Name = lv.Name
ORDER BY sc.stars DESC
LIMIT 5;
```

[38;2;149;165;166m10:49:51[0m | [1m[38;2;155;89;182mORCHESTRATOR[0m | INFO     | [38;2;46;204;113m[1mSUCCESS: Generated SQL executed successfully! (5 rows)[0m
[38;2;149;165;166m10:49:51[0m | [1m[38;2;155;89;182mORCHESTRATOR[0m | INFO     | SQL:
SELECT pi.Name,
       pi.Version,
       stars_info.stars
FROM (
    SELECT pi.Name,
           pi.Version,
           TRY_CAST(json_extract(pi.VersionInfo, '$.Ordinal') AS BIGINT) AS ordinal,
           json_extract_string(pi.VersionInfo, '$.IsRelease') AS is_release,
           pi.System
    FROM packageinfo pi
    WHERE pi.System = 'NPM'
) pi
JOIN project_packageversion pv
  ON pi.Name = pv.Name AND pi.Version = pv.Version
JOIN (
    SELECT TRY_CAST(REPLACE(REGEXP_EXTRACT(Project_Information, '([0-9]+) star', 1), ',', '') AS BIGINT) AS stars,
           REGEXP_EXTRACT(Project_Information, 'project ([^ ]+)', 1) AS repo_name
    FROM project_info
    WHERE REGEXP_EXTRACT(Project_Information, '([0-9]+) star', 1) IS NOT NULL
) stars_info
  ON pv.ProjectName = stars_info.repo_name
WHERE pi.is_release = 'true'
QUALIFY ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY pi.Name ORDER BY pi.ordinal DESC) = 1
ORDER BY stars_info.stars DESC
LIMIT 5;
